# H1 application-error containment on main

Historical main evidence at `b8816902`. Retired paths are rendered as text; see the [native merge port](goway-merge-main-h1-port.md) for current owners and verification.

Base: `7cc0b93`. Read-only inputs: lane A B37 remainder diff/report and
`redaction-inventory-h1.md`. No branch-only ORM APIs were introduced.

## Review correction status

The later `last-3.md` review rejected the implementation recorded below. Its
seven findings supersede the earlier source-review conclusion and acceptance
claim. The prior commands verified the prior source fingerprint only.

Correction sources now contain native List streaming errors (and nested native
Optional/Nullable/JSON method returns), isolate field mutators and autocommit
model decoders, restore legacy SQL-driver cancellation cleanup, and keep enum
round-trip decode causes diagnostic-only. Regression sources are
[value/list_stream_error_test.go](../../value/list_stream_error_test.go),
[database/field_mutator_error_test.go](../../database/field_mutator_error_test.go),
[database/autocommit_decoder_error_test.go](../../database/autocommit_decoder_error_test.go),
[internal/sqlcallback/driver_cancellation_test.go](../../internal/sqlcallback/driver_cancellation_test.go),
and [http/enum_error_classification_test.go](../../http/enum_error_classification_test.go).
The focused correction race batch passed in segments: the first run caught two
incorrect assertions that native JSON must return no partial buffer on error;
the selective rerun passed after aligning those assertions with the native API.
HTTP response-byte comparisons and the broader requested race batch passed.
The lead subsequently approved an explicit Foundry registration contract. The
per-rewrite catalog query is removed. `postgres.Config.RegisterTypes` runs once
per physical connection and receives a scoped `TypeRegistry`; wrapping is memoized
by the original descriptor pointer. A framework-owned encode plan detects native
registration during external rewrites using only public pgx APIs. Unchanged
rewrites preserve the plan; changed registries fail closed and discard the
connection without replaying the callback. No fork or private reflection is used.
Current registration acceptance results are recorded below.

## Classification and compatibility regressions

The following test sources specify the classification and containment assertions.
Their execution results belong in the verification section below.

- `TestBindRedactsEncoderAndValidatorFailures` in
  [database/codec/binding_error_test.go](../../database/codec/binding_error_test.go)
  and `TestDecodeAndScanRedactWithoutReclassifying` in
  [database/codec/decoding_error_test.go](../../database/codec/decoding_error_test.go)
  retain causes without adding Invalid to infrastructure errors.
  `TestCursorCodecRedactionPreservesClassification` in
  `database/query/cursor_codec_redaction_test.go`
  retains the cursor's explicit Invalid classification.
- `TestJSONDiagnosticCausesKeepHTTP500` in
  [http/json_error_classification_test.go](../../http/json_error_classification_test.go)
  compares response bytes with main's error-code responses for direct and wrapped
  JSON failures, explicit outer errors and joined siblings. It covers snapshots,
  decoding and JSON map paths. Its deadline case checks HTTP 500 separately from
  retained `errors.Is` identity.
  `TestParseJSONMapKeyValidationKeepsHTTPClassification` checks the direct
  map-key path's existing HTTP 400 and response bytes.
  `TestJSONDiagnosticCausesDoNotSupplyRetryHints` compares response bytes and
  Retry-After for a diagnostic overload cause. These tests do not claim a
  separate diagnostic test for every authentication or validation error family.
  HTTP byte preservation excludes the approved canonical-key `MarshalText`
  panic/Goexit correction: these return 500 instead of 400 on `e1c2356a`.
  Existing `TestErrorClassificationNeverPublishesInternalMessages` in
  [http/errors_test.go](../../http/errors_test.go) covers ordinary outer and joined
  classification precedence and public response redaction.
- `TestSQLCallbackClassificationKeepsContextPrecedence` and
  `TestSQLCallbacksKeepHostileInspectionOutsideForeignLibraries` in
  [database/sql_callbacks_test.go](../../database/sql_callbacks_test.go)
  cover context precedence and hostile inspection.
  [database/postgres/callback_codec_test.go](../../database/postgres/callback_codec_test.go)
  covers native conversion, query rewriting, type changes and uninferred composites.
- `TestJSONCodecFailuresAreContained`,
  `TestJSONCancellationDoesNotHideCodecFailure` and
  `TestUnionNestedCodecFailureRemainsInternal` in
  [contract/json_test.go](../../contract/json_test.go) and
  [contract/json_union_test.go](../../contract/json_union_test.go) retain DTO and
  union failure classifications. `TestJSONScalarDecodingRedacts` and
  `TestRelationBatchDecodingRedacts` in
  [database/decoding_paths_test.go](../../database/decoding_paths_test.go) cover
  those database decoding consumers.
- [internal/jsoncodec/json_test.go](../../internal/jsoncodec/json_test.go) covers
  callback opacity, panic/Goexit, native method selection, streaming fallback,
  JSON options and nested callback addressability.
  `TestNestedRevealKeepsDiagnosticPruningAndSiblingPrecedence` and
  `TestRevealRejectsOversizedProjectionWithoutTruncatingNativeIdentity` in
  [internal/safeerror/error_test.go](../../internal/safeerror/error_test.go)
  check nested diagnostic boundaries and bounded projection without dropping
  caller-visible causes.
- [database/callback_error_containment_test.go](../../database/callback_error_containment_test.go)
  covers exact transaction error identity, savepoint/session identity, observer
  cleanup, decoder failures with and without hooks, and PatchEach rollback.
  [database/seed/error_containment_test.go](../../database/seed/error_containment_test.go),
  [database/migrate/error_containment_test.go](../../database/migrate/error_containment_test.go),
  [http/pagination/error_containment_test.go](../../http/pagination/error_containment_test.go)
  and [validation/lookup_error_containment_test.go](../../validation/lookup_error_containment_test.go)
  cover hostile consumer error inspection and conservative failure outcomes.

The intentional public addition is `postgres.Config.RegisterTypes` and its scoped
`TypeRegistry`. Existing pool/schema registration remains in its original setup
path. Dependencies and generated formats are unchanged. Internal conversion
adapters add implementation footprint; unchanged byte size is not claimed.
JSON conversion and HTTP error classification contain Goexit instead of
terminating the caller; see [compatibility](../compatibility.md#application-error-containment-h1).

Custom codecs must register through the hook. Rewriters that call native pgx
registration, including a same-pointer registration, fail closed with a safe
fault naming the hook. The native plan cache invalidates on these calls, so the
public sentinel intentionally detects calls rather than attempting private-map
inspection. A failed callback or sentinel check closes the connection, and sealed
failures expose no native retry capability. `TypeRegistry.LoadType` uses pgx's
server discovery during connection setup only, explicitly outside Foundry's
statement observer. It resolves the target under the original path with qualified
casts, scopes pgx's unqualified discovery to `pg_catalog, pg_temp`, and restores
the path before returning. Failure prevents connection admission. The live
startup-path regression shadows both `text` and `pg_type`, checks qualified and
unqualified target names, and checks restoration after a swallowed load failure.
Ordinary statements have no registration catalog query.

The containment guarantee covers framework-owned conversion and inspection.
Application code can call a native library directly inside its own callback,
including a custom MarshalJSONTo or QueryRewriter body. Those application-owned
native calls remain the callback's responsibility. The framework contains the
callback's panic/Goexit and returned failure, and protects later framework-owned
conversion. Foundry's List streaming callback passes its slice through the
protected encoder, preserving its options and element containment.

Registration regression sources cover descriptor pointer memoization, aliases
with equal OIDs and different names, distinct OIDs sharing a name, retained nested
descriptors, hook failures and escaped registry lifetime, protected custom codecs,
no-op plan retention, native-registration rejection, and a shadowing search path.
The benchmark uses ordinary, no-op and real rewrites through the public DB path.
Protocol tracing counts server executions and ReadyForQuery cycles separately
from timing; every timed operation reads and validates the result. It measures
one warm unscoped connection, excluding setup/authentication/initial preparation.

## Registration measurements

The final benchmark passed five runs per case, with `-test.benchtime=500ms`, on
Go 1.27.1, darwin/arm64, Apple M4 Max (16 logical cores), `GOMAXPROCS=4`, using
one warm connection to the configured test PostgreSQL service. Each iteration
executes `ScanOne` and validates 42. Setup, authentication, initial preparation
and protocol tracing are excluded from allocation/timing measurement. Eight
separate warmed protocol samples per case count frontend Query/Execute messages
and backend ReadyForQuery messages without retaining payloads.

| Case | Server statements/op | Protocol cycles/op | Allocs/op, median (range) | B/op, median (range) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Ordinary | 1 | 1 | 143 (143–144) | 7,570 (7,549–7,703) |
| No-op custom rewrite | 1 | 1 | 178 (178–178) | 9,257 (9,234–9,261) |
| SQL/argument rewrite | 1 | 1 | 179 (179–179) | 9,253 (9,252–9,261) |

No-op rewriting adds zero server statements and does not invalidate the retained
encoding plan. It still incurs callback containment and argument-adaptation
allocations; this is not a claim of zero total rewrite overhead. These are public
DB-path measurements, not an isolated sentinel microbenchmark. Scoped pool
checkout checks are excluded by the explicitly unscoped pool.

The saved pre-change binary was attempted both inside and outside the sandbox;
all cases failed during its capture-rewriter warmup. Those failures are not valid
performance samples, so no measured before/after reduction is claimed. Current
latencies are also not interpreted: host load was 24.97/36.74/35.34 at the start
on 16 logical cores, and timings varied substantially. Wire statement counts were stable. Ordinary-query allocations ranged from
143 to 144; both rewritten cases had identical allocation counts across runs. Logs: `registration-benchmark-final.log`,
`registration-baseline-probe-unrestricted.log` in `/private/tmp/foundry-h1`.

The first race attempt exposed an invalid self-recursive native composite test
fixture: native pgx recursively asks its child for a preferred format. The fixture
now exercises descriptor-copy memoization directly without registering an invalid
native format graph; valid alias/composite registration assertions remain. The
full PostgreSQL package rerun passed. A sandboxed live probe also failed to open
the service; the unrestricted retry and final live package run passed. Private
configuration was sourced without logging credentials.

The broader race run uses ordinary opt-in defaults for external services; live
PostgreSQL evidence is the separately required adapter package run above. No
full consumer PostgreSQL integration gate or new footprint measurement is
claimed. The approved registration API is the intentional public addition;
dependencies and generated/persisted formats remain unchanged. No commit was made.

## Importer gate correction on e1c2356a

The lead's full gate exposed a diagnostic regression, not a bypass of the XML
bound. `Definition.Run` returned the whole framework pipeline's error through
`callback.Isolated`; H1's neutral wrapper retained `fault.Invalid` but replaced
“import XLSX contains an oversized value” with “datatable import failed”.

The importer now privately identifies its own input diagnostics before callback
redaction. It restores their safe message and retains the underlying fault and
heading error identities. Application-created faults are not trusted by type:
reader and handler failures remain redacted. No parser limit or rejection is
relaxed. The existing delimiter-bomb test keeps its message assertion and now
also checks `errors.As`, fault code and exact underlying diagnostic. New cases
cover malformed CSV, heading diagnostics, hostile reader/handler `Error` and
`Format` methods (panic/Goexit), and a private application-created fault.

The H1 wrapping sweep traced concrete type assertions and sentinel comparisons
through their callback owners:

| Path | Disposition |
| --- | --- |
| `datatable/importer/importer.go`, `Definition.Run` / `invalid` | Fixed owned-diagnostic loss as described above. |
| `foundation/inspection.go`, `foundation/services.go`, `foundation/registration_errors.go` | The full gate also found hidden duplicate job-owner names. Mark registry-owned diagnostics and restore their messages after provider/override containment; arbitrary provider faults remain redacted. The existing job regression is unchanged; foundation tests assert both top-level text and underlying fault identity. |
| `i18n/load.go`, `Load` / `ReadTemplates` | The resumed full gate found the same whole-pipeline diagnostic loss. Keep framework catalog errors separately from isolation; filesystem failures are already replaced with safe locale/file/key diagnostics at their reader boundary. Existing diagnostic tests stay intact; new hostile filesystem cases protect redaction. |
| `value/json_keys.go`, `CheckJSONKeys`; `contract/json_key_check.go`, `jsonKeyInternalFailure` | Fixed a related classification defect: callback wrapping hid the concrete internal fault from canonical-key encoding. Capture the framework result separately inside containment so internal failure still precedes cancellation/input rejection. Text methods remain contained by the JSON owner. |
| `http/path.go`, `Path.decode` / `pathInternalFailure` | Already keeps codec results separately from callback errors; no change. |
| `auth/scope.go`, `Scope.run`; `auth/policy.go`, `denied` | Already captures bounded `*Denial` classification inside ownership and returns the typed denial afterward; no change. |
| `websocket/cluster_runtime.go`, `clusterOperation` | Already classifies inside the callback and constructs its typed cluster error afterward; no change. |
| `storage/reader.go`, `ownedReader.Read`; `internal/ownedstream/body.go`, `Body.Read`; `http/endpoint_body.go`, `readEndpointBody` | Already captures raw reader results separately, preserving native EOF and concrete size-limit handling; no change. |
| `internal/sqlcallback/driver.go`, `call` | Explicit native driver sentinel preservation plus bounded bad-connection matching already present; no change. |
| `internal/jsoncodec/methods.go`, callback finish/skip handling | Native unsupported-method control and contained panic identity already handled explicitly; no change. |
| Contract decode/encode errors consumed in datatable and HTTP | Typed errors are constructed after callback containment; no change. |
| `jobs/worker_execution.go`, `Worker.finalize`; `cli/arguments.go`, `ParseFlags` / `InvalidArguments` | Ownership-lost is constructed after callback return; flag help is returned directly from the native parser. No change. |

New value and contract cases distinguish round-trip MarshalText panic/Goexit
(internal) from returned errors (invalid, identity retained). The HTTP regression
runs a typed endpoint, compares both failure bodies byte-for-byte with the
standard 500 response, prevents handler invocation, and follows with a healthy
204 request. Source comparison with `7cc0b93a` distinguishes two cases: H1's
newly contained MarshalText Goexit started returning through the nested wrapper
and became input rejection; MarshalText panic already had that nested-wrapper
problem before H1. Both now follow the existing explicit internal-failure
precedence and return standard HTTP 500. Ordinary returned key-codec errors
remain invalid input. This is an approved 400→500 compatibility correction
relative to `e1c2356a`; the byte comparison is against the standard 500 response,
not the previous 400 response. Public APIs and dependencies are unchanged.

Verification uses the same required environment as above. Logs for this
correction are `/private/tmp/foundry-h1/import-fix-*.log`. The changed-Go-source
manifest is `import-fix-source-sha256.txt`, SHA256
`32becaeda9458a7174de97d7fb4408799e8bb7050a7a5c1c36c6f9f208eba2e8`.

diagnostic regressions, then duplicate job-owner diagnostic loss. Both were
fixed at their owning boundaries without weakening the failing tests. The
remaining root-package batches also ran to completion while collecting findings.
The final race run includes every changed package plus the jobs regression.

were unset for this correction. The ordinary gate compiles those test sources;
no new live PostgreSQL, Redis or editor-gopls run is claimed.

## Attachment publication correction and whole-tree contract sweep (d7e4a651)

The lead's live PostgreSQL gate found a safe-message regression in the consumer
`articles.TestPreparedUploadAfterTheSnapshotIsReportedInvisible`. The
`fault.Invalid` and underlying `*fault.Error` survived, but `extensions.Store.Join`
and then `attachments.Collection.publishPrepared` both passed the error through
`workscope.Group.Run` and `callback.Isolated`, replacing its diagnostic each time.
The snapshot bound and rejection itself were intact. The first full PostgreSQL
run of this correction showed that `Savepoint` also joins successful cleanup
with the callback error, hiding the concrete marker before those wrappers.
Publication now captures its immutable owner message before entering that
boundary and reattaches it to the complete returned failure graph, including any
cleanup or unknown-outcome causes. No database transaction semantics changed.

Attachment-owned faults now snapshot their diagnostic at construction using
`safeerror.Owned`. Callback containment recognizes only that private concrete,
immutable marker, prefixes its safe text and retains the original cause. It
neither formats nor traverses application errors. An exposed `*fault.Error` can
still be inspected with `errors.As`; mutating it cannot change the snapshot.
Importer and registry diagnostics use the same owner instead of separate marker
implementations. Arbitrary application faults, including faults returned from
attachment hooks, retain generic redaction. Foreign SQL/JSON seals and diagnostic
classification pruning are unchanged; the marker is not an HTTP diagnostic-only
boundary. No public API or dependency was added.

The sweep searched the complete Go tree (root plus independent fixtures), not
only the H1 diff. Its source inventories are retained in
`/private/tmp/foundry-h1/attachment-wrapper-inventory.txt` (362 original
callback/safeerror sites), `attachment-identity-matches.txt` (direct type/sentinel
matches), and `attachment-message-matches.txt` (256 test message assertions).
Indirect `workscope.Run` and `frameworkadapter.Invoke` owners were traced too.
The matching contracts and their dispositions are grouped below; arbitrary
application diagnostic text is intentionally not a preserved contract.

| Contract / paths reviewed | Disposition |
| --- | --- |

Invisibility now survives both extension-store and attachment operation wrappers. The consumer keeps its original assertion and also checks the exact fault diagnostic, `Unpublished`, absent attachment and operation ID. |
| `attachments/read.go`, `variants.go`: `ActiveContentRefused`, `VariantUnavailable`, batch/original-superseded sentinels | Existing callers use `errors.Is`; H1 wrappers retain these causes. Public sentinel types and values are unchanged. |

Unknown outcomes remain unknown, preserve operation identity and cannot trigger speculative cleanup. Existing live commit/cleanup regressions remain in the full PostgreSQL gate. |

Existing exact-message, classification, identity and mutation-and-return tests remain unchanged. |

Keep the approved canonical-key 400→500 correction. JSON decode/encode rejection types are constructed after containment. |

Lookup, reconciliation and commit classifiers inspect retained causes. AfterCommit causes stay committed and safely redacted. |

Supervised handler markers are constructed outside callback wrapping. No direct identity check now sees a wrapper instead of its marker. |

Retry/loss/overload decisions inspect retained causes within containment; feature failure constructors own externally reported classifications. Application handlers stay redacted. |

New shared-boundary tests exercise nested isolated/synchronous callbacks,
mutated exposed fault causes, hostile Error/Format/Is/As/Unwrap implementations,
exact safe text and retained identity. Marker recognition performs no custom
inspection. New live attachment tests exercise both before/after hooks with
hostile Error/Format implementations (panic and Goexit), retaining the original
fault/cause and classification, with zero formatting calls and no publication.
Attachment-owned cleanup aggregation now retains known immutable diagnostics
without formatting other failures; a lone failure keeps its exact identity.
The live collection-full regression additionally asserts its message after
publication rejection and cleanup. A mutation-and-cleanup test checks mixed

## Review 7 correction scope

This correction adds no public symbols or dependencies. Private wrappers snapshot diagnostics on failure paths; the fixed package-initialization sentinel registry preserves existing public pointer types while keeping their presentation immutable. Those wrappers and source-owner helpers add implementation footprint, so unchanged binary size is not claimed. Successful operations gain no server statements or registration refreshes from this correction.

Review 7 closed the handwritten source inventory that the earlier grouped sweep left incomplete. The current inventory covers direct callbacks, adapter calls, recovery, safe wrappers, generic failure replacements, and `Run` calls. The tables identify lexical sites and shared regression sources. A shared regression does not prove a separate pipeline for every caller.

Framework owners capture text when they construct an error. Generic callback and feature wrappers retain that immutable text. Arbitrary application errors keep generic redaction. Raw faults remain reachable for `errors.Is` and `errors.As`, but their mutable messages and frames are not diagnostic authority. Contained panic and Goexit failures carry owner-issued provenance. Structured logging reads captured notes and frames, while untrusted faults contribute classification only.

`safeerror.Sentinel` preserves the public `*fault.Error` type and captures text at package initialization. Its registry recognizes the exact registered pointer. The source contract restricts registration to direct package-variable initializers. `safeerror.NewFault`, `WrapFault`, and classification-neutral `New` capture other framework diagnostics. `WrapFailure`, `Prefix`, `Join`, and `Retain` preserve captured text around the complete returned cause and outcome graph.

Native SQL and JSON can add their own wrappers. SQL restores captured diagnostics after bounded, isolated inspection, without replacing database classifications or outcomes. JSON captures method diagnostics before native wrapping and restores them at its finishing owner. Session, transaction, after-commit, row, query, middleware, and feature cleanup retain the complete error graph. Committed or unknown outcomes do not become retryable failures to restore a message. Attachment publication unwraps only owned presentation wrappers around the direct storage result. Nested cleanup errors cannot establish that publication was unchanged. Supervised pubsub handler failures keep their terminal private marker through owned presentation wrappers. The supervisor unwraps only those owned wrappers before checking that direct marker, so a handler failure cannot become retryable.

Concrete successful results retain their public metadata contracts. In particular, validation Localize returns public `*Errors` metadata consumed directly by HTTP. That success result is not a returned failure pipeline. Public application-created `fault.Error`, `storage.Failure`, and `database.NewError` values remain untrusted diagnostic sources. The private database presentation owner snapshots known operation and code values after outcome mutation, while preserving a more-specific captured cause diagnostic. Its explicit whitelist contains framework Code constants. Arbitrary adapter or copied custom codes remain untrusted. Public NewError operation text does not enter that owner. Migration action failures deliberately capture the migration name, commit outcome, and recovery instruction with `WrapFault`. Those owner-selected diagnostics take precedence over a generic captured inner database diagnostic. Generic feature failures use `WrapFailure` when the captured child reason remains the useful action detail. Semantic error constructors keep their own actionable diagnostic, while transparent containment retains its source diagnostic. Query `WriteError` therefore snapshots its transaction-outcome reconciliation message instead of replacing it with an inner `CommitUnknown` classification. Its candidate, original causes, and typed outcome remain available unchanged. Built-in CLI usage diagnostics now originate in `internal/clidiag.Usage` or private `cli.ownedUsage`, so runtime command rejections retain their message through the CLI module and foundation kernel. Public `cli.Usage` remains an untrusted application constructor. `InvalidArguments` retains an already-captured marker without granting provenance to arbitrary validator text. Built-in enum failures use their source owners: email literal whitelists, auth WithCause for registered builtin codes, idempotency failure, and websocket codeFailure for known protocol codes. Arbitrary enum values and Error methods remain untrusted. Public email Classification results remain concrete Kind data, while private jobs payloadFailure snapshots each fresh payloadError diagnostic. Protocol decoder and cluster control checks unwrap only owned presentation wrappers before direct Code decisions. Cookie, URL-signing, and local-storage source sentinels are snapshotted at the emitting owner. The enum source guard discovers typed string Error constants from their declarations and checks direct error-result positions, including callbacks. It excludes classification data and respects local shadows rather than maintaining a duplicated enum list. Framework owners capture only diagnostics they construct, and no public API was added.

The source inventory is complete within the explicit scan scope below. Regression links identify shared contract sources and dedicated pipeline sources. They do not establish a complete test per lexical caller. The current full verification and required PostgreSQL gates passed; results are recorded below. Historical passing gates above apply only to their recorded revisions.

### Inventory scope and reproduction

The historical Review 7 AST scan read 1,542 handwritten runtime Go files. It records **1,375 handwritten lexical boundary entries** and **280 generated wrapper entries**, for **1,655 total lexical boundary entries**. The handwritten total includes the explicitly labeled `Run` call candidates. The generated total consists of 276 `Prefix` calls and 4 `WrapFault` calls.

The separate construction catalogue contains **5,503 handwritten** and **528 generated** construction sites. Construction and boundary indexes overlap. Every `WrapFault`, `WrapFailure`, `Owned`, and `Prefix` call is indexed as both a construction and a wrapper boundary, including `WrapFault` calls with non-nil cause expressions and `Owned` snapshots around a cause. `NewFault`, `New`, `Sentinel`, and `Panic` are construction sites. A constructor is also a generic-replacement candidate when its literal text matches that category. The totals are not added across these different roles. The catalogue also records 1 direct named-error enum return or assignment sites separately. Those source-emission entries are not constructor calls or containment boundaries. Error-returning enum factory methods are listed as both factory construction calls and wrapper boundaries; their source owner, not the receiver type or method name, determines provenance. A typed-error literal is a construction candidate, not proof that its message should replace an intentional public response.

The handwritten boundary classes overlap as follows:

| Lexical class | Sites |
| --- | ---: |
| CLI usage classification wrapper | 6 |
| Run call candidate | 85 |
| SQL capsule/recovery | 30 |
| adapter | 4 |
| bounded foreign diagnostic recovery | 2 |
| callback | 308 |
| classified wrapper | 6 |
| generic replacement candidate | 110 |
| named-error enum factory wrapper | 279 |
| recovery | 4 |
| safe wrapper/aggregation | 602 |

The scan includes function literals in package initializers, same-package helper calls, and all selector `Run` calls. A `Run` row is a candidate owner call, not proof of a callback by method name alone. Classification callbacks that return nil with a captured result stay in the index. Constructor calls resolve imports by their Go import paths, including renamed imports. Typed-error literal detection uses declared names ending in `Error` and the shared private error representations. The construction index also records designated CLI usage helpers and free-function error factories that forward a `message string` parameter to a known constructor or another discovered factory. This includes literal callsites for validation, attachment, registration, importer, and other message-forwarding helpers. A factory-call label does not grant trusted provenance or claim that every domain constructor has been inferred. A separate AST pass resolves primitive named Error types, typed constants and aliases, callback error-result positions, explicit error assignments, and receiver-resolved error-returning enum methods. It excludes Code-valued data returns such as email Classification. The source catalogue also lists error-returning free functions with enum-typed parameters as factory candidates, covering Code helpers and their literal caller arguments. Public factories and conditional whitelists do not acquire ownership merely from these labels. This is lexical type resolution rather than field-sensitive data-flow analysis through arbitrary interfaces. This lexical index does not replace call-graph analysis or runtime evidence.

The scanner parses 62 generated runtime files separately. It excludes 1,531 test-support and fixture source files from runtime claims, and records 1,004 runtime test files only for regression names. The scanner also reads 67 tooling test files for generator regression names. Test sources are not executed by the inventory. The runtime inventory also excludes `internal/cachetest`, `internal/extensiontest`, `internal/jobtest`, `internal/leasetest`, `internal/lockouttest`, `internal/outboxtest`, `internal/pubsubtest`, and `internal/ratelimittest` as test helpers. The runtime source contract separately excludes `tests`, `testkit`, `internal/testinputs`, `internal/scheduletest`, `internal/websockettest`, `tools`, `internal/generate`, and `internal/cmd`. Hidden directories, dependency vendors, and node_modules are outside this repository-owned Go scan.

The reproducible task artifacts are `/private/tmp/h1-inventory.go`, `/private/tmp/h1-inventory.json`, `/private/tmp/h1-source-diagnostics.md`, and `/private/tmp/h1-evidence.py`. Run `GOCACHE=/private/tmp/foundry-mainfix-gocache2 GOMAXPROCS=4 GOFLAGS=-p=4 GOWORK=off go run /private/tmp/h1-inventory.go` at the repository root, then `python3 /private/tmp/h1-evidence.py` to refresh this section. These commands parse source and write inventories. They do not run framework tests or acceptance gates.

Runtime source fingerprint, covering paths and bytes of handwritten and generated runtime Go files, is `cdd6029a603e430de56adb4d54828c9698b7fb4dadd12b6e7bc92b59937c3db3`. Test source and tooling are excluded from this fingerprint. Source edits after this snapshot invalidate these counts.

### Generated diagnostics

The regenerated framework output has no raw fault constructor in this scan. Framework-generated diagnostics use their internal owner. ## Review 7 containment site inventory

Each row is one lexical call or recovery site in the scanned handwritten runtime. The family column identifies the local source package, while callbacks can return diagnostics from the entire [diagnostic catalogue](main-h1-diagnostic-sources.md). The regression column identifies a shared boundary contract or a dedicated public-path source. Repeated regression names denote shared coverage, not separate successful test executions. The lexical count is not an integration-test count.

### Review 7 generated wrapper boundary inventory

These emitted wrappers are runtime H1 boundaries and are included in the total above. Each `Prefix` call retains the static generated context around its cause. Each `WrapFault` call snapshots the generated representation diagnostic and retains its cause. The emitter regression proves framework-versus-application code generation. Shared runtime boundary sources establish the source-marker behavior. These links do not claim one integration test per emitted method.

### Direct enum emission disposition

The separate source-emission index contains one direct enum return: the default branch of [WebSocket codeFailure](../../websocket/code_diagnostics.go). Its sixteen known protocol constants use the owned branch. The default intentionally returns an unknown or application-supplied Code without granting provenance. It is not an unmarked built-in constant source. No bare built-in enum constant return or explicit error assignment remains in this AST snapshot. The declaration-driven source guard needs no return-site allowlist.

reused after the user's rotation instruction. Runtime fingerprint:
`cdd6029a603e430de56adb4d54828c9698b7fb4dadd12b6e7bc92b59937c3db3`.

The affected-package race checks passed in retained segments before the full
gates. The first command covered CLI, audit commands, jobs, email, authentication,

reach malformed-reference validation, and its focused race rerun passed:
`go test -race -timeout=20m ./storage/s3 -run '^TestS3SourceCodeDiagnosticsSurviveContainment$'`.
Logs: `review7-late-source-race-cache2.log`,
`review7-owner-race-rerun-cache2.log`, and
`review7-s3-source-rerun-cache2.log`, all under `/private/tmp/foundry-h1`.

Earlier attempts are retained as failure history, not final acceptance:

- The first verification attempt exposed the supervised pubsub terminal-marker
  loss. Its correction passed, but that source preceded the final database and
  enum-owner corrections. The first PostgreSQL attempt was interrupted during
  consumer fixtures when the user rotated the cache.
- Cache2 acceptance exposed migration action-context loss and WriteError
  reconciliation-context loss. Their owners now preserve those actionable outer
  diagnostics while retaining exact inner messages and typed outcome/candidate
  identity. Focused migration and observer race reruns passed; no outcome or
  formatting assertions were removed.
- A mixed-source verification attempt reached generation while `internal/emaildiag`
  was being added and failed to load that unfinished package. It is not counted
  as final acceptance.
- The new source guard rejected remaining raw built-in enum returns. Those
  construction sites were fixed without adding exceptions. The first affected
  race run also found an accidental WebSocket helper filename collision; the
  original diagnostics implementation was restored and the helper moved to
  `code_diagnostics.go`. The corrected source guard and affected packages passed.
- The S3 fixture initially exercised unprepared-backend validation rather than
  malformed cleanup-reference validation. Its corrected setup preserves all
  message, classification, identity and zero-formatting assertions.

The historical Review 7 inventory contains **1,655 lexical boundary entries** (1,375 handwritten,
280 generated). These are call/recovery sites, including explicitly labeled
`Run` candidates, not a claim of 1,655 independent integration tests. Shared
boundary tables and dedicated pipeline tables are linked above. The source
contract rejects raw fault constructors, unsafe cleanup joins, unowned built-in
CLI usage and direct built-in enum errors; the only remaining direct enum
emission is the intentional untrusted custom-code fallback described above.

All changes remain uncommitted.


## Review 8 corrections (current worktree)

The previous lexical inventory and passing gates did not prove exhaustive
containment. Review 8 identified reopened graphs and untrusted diagnostic
providers as well as three wrapper regressions. The source guard now rejects raw
`errors.New`/`fmt.Errorf` outside explicit construction/presentation owners, checks
named enum-result assignments and initializer callbacks, and requires a counted
owner disposition for typed error literals. These checks enforce reviewed forms;
they are not a formal proof of arbitrary dataflow.

Corrections: terminal-only JSON fallback messages; fault snapshot deduplication by
construction identity; asset refusal reporting; protected cache/config JSON;
normalized object validation; immutable database attribute snapshots and HTTP
code whitelisting; bounded isolated matching across importer, extension loaders,
settings, attachment/database consumers, storage transfer and local backend,
SMTP, CLI input, HTTP bodies/SSE/session, password and WebSocket classification.
S3 cleanup aggregates retained failures without inspecting them to deduplicate.
Enum wrappers keep original enum nodes for `errors.As`.

Adversarial self-review also found matching after containment in translation and
attachment slots, schedule operations, idempotency pruning, storage HTTP adapters,
HTTP flush/webhook adapters and authentication rehashing. These now use the shared
contained matchers. Native-only filesystem and scalar-only JSON calls remain
native; application JSON methods use the protected JSON owner. Framework error
formatters may format only their own concrete fields or captured diagnostics.

### Intentional CLI presentation exception

Public `cli.Report` deliberately renders the supplied error using `Error()` inside
callback isolation. Existing tests and the public command-line contract require
that operator-facing text. This exception does not authorize application text in
HTTP/logging/structured reports. Claims of no application error formatting in this
evidence refer to runtime containment and redacted reporting, excluding this
explicit presentation API and command-line tool diagnostics.

### Review 8 acceptance

`make verify` passed as a segmented run. The user retired `gocache2` during
the root tests; all remaining Go commands use
`GOCACHE=/private/tmp/foundry-mainfix-gocache3`, `GOMAXPROCS=4`, `GOFLAGS=-p=4`,
`GOWORK=off`. Completed toolchain/format/vet and 76 root-package results were
retained; the 165 remaining root packages resumed with the same six-package
batching and 20-minute timeout. Fixture vet/tests, generated-output checks,
documentation checks and release-tool checks then passed. The four owners edited
after the initial vet phase were vetted again, followed by format and diff checks.
The real editor probes used the existing approved gopls executable and passed.

The complete required-mode `make test-postgres` race gate passed (exit 0) on
`gocache3`: all root and independent consumer packages ran with `-race`,
`-count=1`, `-mod=readonly`, six-package batches and a 20-minute package timeout.
The existing private test URL was exported from the primary worktree's
`.env.test` without printing it, with `FOUNDRY_TEST_POSTGRES_REQUIRED=1`.
This includes the importer and article-publication regressions. No runtime or
test source changed during either completed gate. Historical Review 7 passes
are not used as acceptance evidence for this revision.

Commands and terminal results:

- `make verify`: PASS, segmented at the user-requested cache rotation. Completed
  work was retained; only unfinished root-test batches and subsequent targets
  resumed. The resume ended with `SEGMENTED MAKE VERIFY COMPLETE` and exit 0.
- `make test-postgres`: PASS, full required-mode race run, exit 0. Log:
  `/private/tmp/foundry-h1/review8-postgres-cache3.log`.
- Final documentation-only updates: `make docs-check` and `git diff --check`.

Earlier iteration failures were corrected before these gates: unused imports,
source-guard dispositions, unknown database-code presentation, and a regression
fixture missing the required validation message. No assertion or guarantee was
weakened. The initial importer fixture failure was rerun successfully before the
completed gate. All edits remain uncommitted.

The self-review additionally closed HTTP-client retry-on-incomplete-inspection,
Redis timeout classification and logging-sink errno inspection. Database slow
logs no longer publish arbitrary adapter code/state strings; query observers keep
their existing typed event contract. These are separate from error presentation.

methods terminate: callback ownership still waits for their actual exit.

## Review 10 corrections

All rows of `last-10.md` were read. The seven closed review-9 findings remain
unchanged; this delta addresses the two remaining owners.

| Finding | Root correction | Real-entry regression |
| --- | --- | --- |
| P1 Redis TLS delegates | A connection-owned TLS boundary isolates and seals Rand, KeyLogWriter, certificate Public/Sign/SignMessage (including certificates returned by GetClientCertificate), Time, and session-cache Get/Put. It retains the first delegate failure independently of native TLS's returned text. The owned dialer closes failed handshakes; guarded reads/writes cover TLS 1.3 session tickets after the handshake and close a failed connection. Ordinary native transport errors retain native inspection behavior when no delegate failed. | `redis/TestStartContainsTLSDelegates`: actual Prepare/Start against a local TLS server; returned private cyclic errors where supported, panic/Goexit, static/selected signers, message signers, Public, clock, cache Get, and TLS 1.2/1.3 cache Put. Assertions include cause identity, panic classification, zero application formatting, and released client ownership. Existing peer/connection/selection regressions remain. |
| P2 JSON child metadata | Each opaque callback capsule carries immutable diagnostic/panic metadata. Discovery walks only the actual returned graph under the shared node/depth bounds and callback isolation, without trusting custom Is/As to attribute metadata. No mutable last-child slot or retained per-operation registry remains. Returning an earlier child or a join preserves its trusted panic classification and every returned cause; handled children contribute nothing. | `http/TestNestedJSONChildrenKeepHTTP500`: actual typed JSON endpoint and direct descriptor decoding, two children, panic/Goexit followed by an ordinary private error; returning first/join has exact HTTP 500 bytes, returning second stays 400, handling both succeeds. `internal/jsoncodec/TestMultipleNestedJSONFailuresRetainReturnedMetadata` covers the encoder counterpart, identity and zero formatting. |

TLS containment covers the public client delegates above. The ECH rejection
callback is wrapped, but no live ECH handshake is claimed. The CHANGELOG names
these tested paths and this limitation explicitly. Public signatures are unchanged.
TLS needs connection ownership beyond dialing because native code can replace
error identities and invoke session delegates during later reads; the wrapper
retains only the first delegate failure and never shares it across connections.
JSON stores fixed-size metadata on returned capsules; graph discovery uses the
existing 256-node/64-depth bound and fails conservatively on incomplete inspection.

the shared safe join and reveals the combined graph before restoring the captured
panic metadata. Focused race reruns passed. Logs are
`/private/tmp/foundry-h1/review10-race.log` and `review10-race-rerun.log`.

## H1 follow-ups: Redis TLS delegate coverage and Read/Write per-I/O isolation

The round 4 follow-ups address per-I/O overhead on the Redis hot path, connection closing behavior on timeouts, and delegate regression coverage.

### 1. Read/Write isolation analysis and per-delegate containment

In `redis/tls.go:81-94`, each `Read` and `Write` on `containedTLSConn` previously invoked `callback.Isolated("Redis TLS operation", ...)`. This started a new goroutine and allocated a channel per I/O call on the Redis hot path.

Analysis of Go's `crypto/tls` callbacks confirms that all client delegates are already individually wrapped:
- `Rand`: wrapped in `tlsReader` using `b.protect`.
- `KeyLogWriter`: wrapped in `tlsWriter` using `b.protect`.
- Certificate private keys: wrapped in `tlsSigner` using `b.protect` (for `Sign` and `SignMessage`) and `b.require` (for `Public`). Client keys are only used for signing, so `crypto.Decrypter`-only keys remain unwrapped with an explicit comment at `tls.go`.
- `Time`: wrapped using `b.require`.
- `ClientSessionCache`: wrapped in `tlsSessionCache` using `b.require` for both `Get` and `Put`.
- `VerifyPeerCertificate`, `VerifyConnection`, `EncryptedClientHelloRejectionVerify`, and `GetClientCertificate`: wrapped using `b.protect`.

No application callbacks run inside `Read` or `Write` that are not already individually contained. Therefore, the per-I/O `callback.Isolated` wrapper was removed from `Read` and `Write`, retaining individual per-delegate containment.

### 2. Connection closing on delegate failure vs. ordinary timeout/EOF

`containedTLSConn.Read` and `Write` now close the raw connection only when a delegate failure has been recorded (`c.boundary.hasFailure()`) or when a panic occurred during the operation (`panicked`). Ordinary `io.EOF` and read timeouts (`net.Error.Timeout() == true`) do not close the underlying raw connection, leaving it open and usable for subsequent commands or pool reuse.

### 3. Regressions

The following real-entry regressions were added to [redis/tls_containment_test.go](../../redis/tls_containment_test.go):
- `TestReadTimeoutLeavesConnectionUsable`: verifies at the connection level that setting a read deadline produces a timeout without closing the raw connection, and subsequent writes and reads succeed.
- `TestClientReadTimeoutLeavesConnectionUsable`: verifies at the `Client` level that a stalled ping with context timeout leaves the client usable for subsequent operations.
- `TestDelegateFailsAfterStartupOnReconnect`: verifies that after successful initial client startup, post-startup reconnects that encounter delegate failures (signer in returned and panic modes, session cache in panic mode, and clock in panic mode) preserve error identity, retain panic classification, format zero application strings, and release client ownership cleanly.
- `TestDelegateFailsOnClose`: verifies that connection close with an underlying delegate failure returns the sealed failure and reveals identity under Foundry classification.
- `TestEncryptedClientHelloRejectionVerifyContainment`: unit test exercising the wrapped `EncryptedClientHelloRejectionVerify` in returned-error, panic, and `runtime.Goexit` modes, verifying error identity, panic classification, and absence of private string disclosure.

### 4. Benchmark measurements: `BenchmarkTLSReadWrite`

Benchmark conditions: darwin/arm64, Apple M4 Max (16 logical cores), `GOMAXPROCS=4`, `GOCACHE=/private/tmp/foundry-mainfix-gocache4`, `GOFLAGS=-p=4`, `GOWORK=off`. Sequential TLS echo over loopback TCP with 64-byte payload, 5 runs with `-benchmem -count=5`.

| Metric | Before (per-I/O `callback.Isolated`) | After (per-delegate containment) | Delta |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Latency (range) | 21,118 – 28,578 ns/op | 15,087 – 16,121 ns/op | -28.6% median latency |
| Latency (median) | 22,175 ns/op | 15,836 ns/op | -6,339 ns/op |
| Allocations | 28 allocs/op | 0 allocs/op | **-100% (-28 allocs/op)** |
| Memory | 1,216 B/op | 0 B/op | **-100% (-1,216 B/op)** |

Per-I/O allocations in `containedTLSConn.Read` and `Write` are completely eliminated (0 allocs/op, 0 B/op).
